ParlaMint-DK_20160512220354	The debate is open and I give the floor to the Minister for the Environment and Food.
ParlaMint-DK_20160512220414	Thank you for that, President, and thank you for the alternative to the motion for a resolution. With the proposal, the focus is on those farmers who want to fertilise optimally in order to increase production compared to then having to take land out of rotation according to the Alternative. The government believes that the resolution should be rejected, and we do so for three reasons. It would cost valuable production areas if a farmer were to take land out of rotation if he took advantage of the possibility of a fertilising up to economic optimum. This also applies to farms far from vulnerable nature, so it is a proposal for general regulation completely without targeting. The proposal therefore goes directly against the wishes of the government. The government will ensure good production conditions while at the same time promoting environmental protection. Where the alternative proposal will provide a rigid regulatory framework, the government will provide flexibility so that the individual farmer can choose the means that give the most effect precisely on his farm it is targeting. The alternative has an unmanageable focus on nature and environmental considerations in Produktionsdanmark. On the other hand, the government will improve nature and agricultural production conditions, as agreed in the form of the Food and Agriculture Package of December 2015. There is a balance in the government package between the phasing out of the reduction of fertiliser standards and the measures introduced to compensate for the phasing-out. In addition, the instruments put in place and planned in the Food and Agriculture Package are considerably more cost-effective than the alternative here proposed. This applies, among other things, to: for wetlands and afforestation. The alternative suggests that if a farmer takes advantage of the removal of the 20% norm reduction. on a part of the holding, he shall at the same time take a different area equal to 20%. of the area where the increased standards are used. The adoption of the alternative proposal puts Danish agriculture in a situation such as the one before phasing out the reduced standards, as it would be advantageous for the farmer not to increase nitrogen consumption on selected land, but only to continue in accordance with previous practice before phasing out the standard reduction. Implementation of the proposal will therefore mean that the phasing out of the norm reduction will not be implemented in practice and that the potential for extending the raw material base and total food production will not be exploited. It remains important to protect and promote the resilience of vulnerable natural areas. The alternative proposal is not targeted but will lead to a blind set-aside of existing agricultural land. The set-aside will apply to all farmers, regardless of whether their land is near natural areas, which may benefit from additional protection. It will have serious economic consequences to force farmers to set aside agricultural land, so that it cannot be fertilised, sprayed or remodelled. The Government does not agree with the alternative that the way forward will be to limit the production potential of the industry. This will not benefit the development of production Denmark, but will result in a reduction in raw material production. As a result, there could be a fall in exports of food products. The proposal will therefore cost Denmark a great deal of money in the social economy, because total agricultural production will fall. With the food and agriculture package of December 2015, the government and the other blue parties concluded an agreement that takes account of the development potential of the industry and at the same time takes account of nature. The package includes: efforts to promote the quality of the aquatic environment and to counter the potential consequences of an increase in the permitted fertiliser consumption of farmers. This is done through targeted measures, including: in the form of mini-marine areas and afforestation. Finally, more nitrogen measurements will provide the necessary background information on where there are challenges with nitrogen leaching. This lays the foundations for targeted and concrete environmental initiatives as well as for targeted nitrogen regulation for the benefit of both the environment and nature and for the benefit of productivity. It is clear to everyone that there are major differences in B 124 and the way the government wants to influence developments in nature and the environment. The Food and Agriculture Package has agreed a fundamental change of regulation away from general and animal and ineffective regulation towards more targeted and cost-effective regulation. Actions must be carried out in the areas where there is a need. Contrary to the alternative, the Government wishes to promote the continued development of agriculture, which will maintain jobs in the production sector and thus maintain the high level of food production for consumers and exports. Targeted regulation of the use of nitrogen in agriculture, taking into account the need for increased protection of vulnerable nature, will allow simultaneous consideration of agricultural production. By using cost-effective instruments, resources can be used optimally and with minimal impact on nature. The targeted regulation will start to be phased in from 2018. Approx. 60% of Denmark's area has been used for agricultural production. The agricultural area has been reduced for many years due to an increase in forest roads and cities. Nevertheless, the industry has succeeded in maintaining the volume of production. Technology development and focus on efficiency, productivity and cost reduction are at the heart of this process. With the Food and Agriculture Package and the forthcoming implementation of targeted regulation, agricultural productivity and competitiveness will continue to be promoted. We are a prosperous country. This is partly due to the fact that we have large and efficient agricultural production. We must continue to work for this, and that is why we reject the alternative proposal.

ParlaMint-DK_20160512224328	Thank you for that, and we'll be home soon. The proposal we have discussed today has been an attempt to respond to targeted regulation in a directly constructive way. This is a proposal that is part of our nature package of 42 proposals, and the two proposals we have dealt with here tonight are both from that package. The idea of this is precisely to say that if we want to fertilize more on certain areas, we could make a direct compensation on the land of each farmer, so that the farmer himself will assess whether he wants to fertilise 3%. more, if he wants to fertilize 10%. more or maybe up to 20%. More. Then he takes out corresponding areas and thus spares nature, because the areas that are taken will typically be wet areas or those that are shaded by any forest or otherwise lie up to nature. This is an instrument which, as such, is also recognised by the researchers and which, of course, takes account of the very problem that the agricultural package has only accounted for 30%. nitrogen reaching the sea, while the agricultural package is not properly accounted for or compensated for the 70% that is often detained along the way. So will this device. So we believe that it is a bid for a very direct instrument, which is precisely an example of how good targeted regulation could be made, which balances the need for more fertilization where the areas can tolerate it, and with less fertiliser emissions close to nature. Thank you for a good debate. I look forward to discussing it a little more in committee. I see it as a greater part of our nature package work here on the side of the Alternative.
